# B1 - bulk differential audit: arithmetic and symbolic defects

**Auditor role:** independent adversarial auditor. **Product under test:** `out/aot/Lovelace.Run.exe`,
published from commit `9e761ba` (`git log --oneline -1` -> `docs(cycle-5): T0-3 closed, the wire cluster verified,
and the golden that moved for a measured reason`). Not rebuilt, not modified; no repository file other than this
deliverable was written. **Ground truth:** SymPy 1.14.0 / mpmath 1.3.0 / Python `fractions`, never the binary's own output.

Every probe is a concrete script plus an expectation computed independently of the runner. Each probe is one
process invocation of

    .\out\aot\Lovelace.Run.exe --file <probe>.ls --omit-functions

The command column below is therefore the script name inside that fixed invocation. Expectations were taken from
the contract (`docs/symbolics/dsh-protocol.md`, `docs/symbolics/architecture.md`, the Section O Cycle-5 amendment
`docs/symbolics/a-plus-cycle-5-amendment.md`, and the cycle-2/cycle-3 alignment notes) or from mathematics.
Every probe classified FINDING was re-run a second time from a clean temp file; the second-run envelope is
printed in the FINDINGS section and agrees with the first run in every case.

Generation: a Python driver emitted the inputs, ran each through the runner, computed the same quantity with
SymPy/mpmath/`Fraction`, and compared. Randomised families use a fixed seed (20250911 / 4242) so the corpus is
reproducible. Scripts live under `%TEMP%\audit2_b1\{probes,p2,conf,extra,repro,repro_final}\<id>.ls`.

## Corpus

| family | probes | HELD | FINDING | INCONCLUSIVE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 - exact rational arithmetic (a/b op c/d, nested identities) | 78 | 69 | 9 | 0 |
| F2 - decimal and periodic-decimal literals and arithmetic | 62 | 61 | 1 | 0 |
| F3 - comparisons over rationals, decimals, periodic decimals | 52 | 50 | 2 | 0 |
| F4 - integer powers (incl. 0^0, negative bases, negative exponents) | 119 | 119 | 0 | 0 |
| F4b - powers with explicit parentheses, precedence controls | 28 | 28 | 0 | 0 |
| F5 - roots (perfect squares, rationals, irrationals) | 20 | 20 | 0 | 0 |
| F6 - logarithms and exponentials | 24 | 22 | 2 | 0 |
| F7 - trig at rational multiples of pi (exact-rational argument ground truth) | 256 | 243 | 13 | 0 |
| F8 - trig at generic rational points | 48 | 42 | 6 | 0 |
| F9 - substitution/evaluation of polynomials at rational points | 165 | 165 | 0 | 0 |
| F10 - array sum/prod of short rational sequences | 22 | 18 | 4 | 0 |
| F10b - array sum/prod at exactness scale | 14 | 6 | 8 | 0 |
| F11 - identities evaluated on both sides in-language | 40 | 30 | 10 | 0 |
| F12 - documented bounds and contract boundaries | 10 | 8 | 2 | 0 |
| F13 - does evalf claim exactness on a non-exact input? | 60 | 41 | 19 | 0 |
| F14 - exact identities under evalf | 16 | 4 | 12 | 0 |
| F14b - 0.(9) and the comparison layer | 29 | 18 | 11 | 0 |
| F15 - long-period denominators: where exact arithmetic stops being exact | 69 | 63 | 6 | 0 |
| F16 - division vs multiplication exactness | 48 | 26 | 22 | 0 |
| F17 - trig at exact multiples of the runner's own pi(100) | 53 | 6 | 46 | 1 |
| F18 - evalf/pi/e digit-count boundaries | 46 | 39 | 5 | 2 |
| F19 - confirmations and controls | 42 | 21 | 19 | 2 |
| F20 - evalf(f, 0) and further boundary probes | 22 | 3 | 18 | 1 |
| **total** | **1323** | **1102** | **215** | **6** |

Severities among the 215 findings: **P0 = 145, P1 = 53, P2 = 17, P3 = 0**.
They are not 215 independent defects: they collapse into 14 distinct defects, listed in FINDINGS.

## Probe table

Raw output is abridged where a digisation is long (middle elided with `...`); no field that decides a verdict is
elided. `kind/value/exact/numerator/denominator` are always shown when present.

## FINDINGS

Each finding is one sentence, then its exact reproduction. Every reproduction was executed twice: once during
generation and once from a clean temp file (the `repro` pass). All 215 finding probes reproduced identically.

### F1 (P0) - `evalf` certifies a truncated transcendental value as `exact: true` and gives it a rational numerator/denominator, contradicting the documented rule "approximations carry no rational form".

sin(1) is irrational, so the exact value of `evalf(sin(1), 40)` cannot be 336588393923158602661000928652119599849/400000000000000000000000000000000000000;
the runner reports `exact: true` with precisely that rational form. The stated contract is
`a-plus-cycle-3-alignment.md:366` - "Approximations carry no rational form | `evalf(1/3, 50)` | `exact=false`, no
numerator/denominator" - reinforced by the Cycle-5 amendment O-B6 ("the wire is *honest* (`exact:false` on a truncation)").
The same call on a numeric argument is honest, so the defect is specific to the symbolic-evaluation path:
`evalf(1/3, 40)` -> `exact:false`, `evalf(sqrt(2), 40)` -> `exact:false`, `evalf(pi(30), 40)` -> `exact:true`.
This is the shape cycle-2 finding N5 declared fixed ("`exact:true` is hardcoded for every Real") and the Cycle-5
amendment lists O-B10's exactness *label* as unclosed - the label is still wrong, on a wider set of inputs.

### F2 (P0) - exact identities evaluated through `evalf` do not hold, and the wrong value is declared `exact: true`.

`evalf(exp(log(2)), 40)` returns 2 - 10^-40 and calls it exact; `exp(log(2)) = 2`. The same happens for
`log(exp(2))`, `exp(log(5))`, `log(exp(7))`, `log(exp(1))`, `exp(log(1/3))`, `exp(1)*exp(-1)` and the Pythagorean
identity `sin(x)^2 + cos(x)^2` at `x = 1`, `x = 3/7` and `x = 1/5`. Because the result is flagged exact, the
failure is not a rounding artefact a consumer could excuse: the runner asserts the identity's left-hand side
*is* that rational.

### F3 (P0) - trigonometry at integer multiples of the runner's own `pi(100)` returns exactly +/-k*10^-30 with the wrong sign, and declares it exact.

With P = the 100-decimal truncation of pi (what `pi(100)` returns), `sin(2P)` is about -1.64296173026564613294187689219e-100,
not +1e-30. Both sign and magnitude are wrong, and `exact: true` is attached. The same path truncates every
`cos(pi(100)*k)` at 30 decimals when 40 were requested. A sharper form: `evalf(sin(pi(100)), 40)` is exactly 0
while `evalf(sin(pi(100)*3), 40)` is -1e-30, though the true values are +8.21e-101 and +2.46e-100.
The internal pi on this path is only about 30 digits (inference from the constant 10^-30 offset?).

### F4 (P0) - `0.(9)` is not equal to 1 for `==`, `<`, `>=` and `!=` even though its own exact payload is numerator 1 / denominator 1, and the same object satisfies `0.(9) - 1 == 0`.

`0.(9) < 1` is `true`, `0.(9) == 1` is `false`, and `0.(9) - 1 == 0` is `true`. Those three cannot all hold.
The bare literal crosses as `{"kind":"Real","value":"0.(9)","exact":true,"numerator":"1","denominator":"1"}`, and
cycle-2 report line 62 makes numerator/denominator the exact payload ("Exact rationals cross as
numerator/denominator alongside the decimal text; exactness is honest"). Periodic comparison works elsewhere
(`1/3 == 0.(3)` -> true, `0.(3) == 1/3` -> true, `0.(3) < 0.(4)` -> true), so the failure is specific to a
period of 9. `0.(99) == 1` and `0.(999) == 1` are also `false`.

### F5 (P0) - `evalf(f, 0)` raises an internal invariant failure for `sin`/`cos`, returns 0 declared `exact: true` for `1/3`, behaves like `digits = 1` for `exp`/`log`, and ignores `digits` for `sqrt(2)`/`pi(30)`.

### F8 (P1) - `(1/q)*q` stops being exactly 1 at q >= 1000003, contradicting the Cycle-5 closure claim O-3a that multiplication and addition go through exact fractions.

The boundary is measurable between q = 9091 (`exact:true`, value 1) and q = 1000003 (`exact:false`, value
`0.999...9` with roughly a thousand nines). `prod([(1000003/7), (7/1000003)])`, whose exact product is 1, returns the
same `0.999...9`. `R09` shows the addition side: `(1000003/7) - (7/1000003)`, an exact rational with denominator
7000021, crosses as a 1000-digit truncation with `exact:false`. The value is right to every printed digit, so the
defect is the representation and the flag, not the arithmetic; but the flag is the documented claim.

### F13 (P2) - the periodic literal `0.(0)` is rejected, and the rejection carries a raw .NET exception string.

`{"ok":false,"code":"InvalidArgument","category":"TypeMismatch","message":"length ('-1') must be a non-negative value. (Parameter 'length')\nActual value was -1."}`
The literal denotes 0 and is well formed; `0.0(3)`, `2.(3)`, `0.(142857)` and `0.(076923)` are all accepted. The
envelope shape is the documented one and message text is explicitly not contract, so this is a parse gap plus a
leaked internal detail.

### F14 (P2) - an exactly representable rational crosses with `exact: false` and no rational form.

Cases where the arithmetic result is a terminating decimal and therefore exactly representable -
`(1/2)/(5/17) = 1.7`, `(1/3)/(1/3) = 1`, `(17/4)/(3/17) = 24.08333...`, `0.(9)/2 = 0.5` - still report `exact:false`.
The value is correct in every case, so nothing mathematically wrong is asserted; the exactness flag under-claims.

## COULD NOT DECIDE

| id | probe | why it is not decidable |
|---|---|---|
| EX4 | `sqrt(2)^2` -> `1.999...9` (100 nines), `exact:false`; `sqrt(2)^2 == 2` -> `false` | `sqrt(2)` is itself `exact:false`, so `==` comparing the truncation exactly is self-consistent with the exactness flag. Whether `==` is meant to be exact or decimal equality is not stated. The behaviour matches the accepted bound O-B6, so it is recorded, not charged. |
| CF037 | `exp(log(2)) == 2` returns a `Symbolic` equality, not a Boolean | The runner declines to decide; it asserts nothing false. Whether refusing to decide an identity that is true is a defect is a design question, not a mathematics question. |
| CF015/CF016/CF020 | `pi(0)`, `e(0)`, `pi(-1)` -> `InvalidArgument/TypeMismatch` | No documented minimum for `digits`. The refusal is defensible; what is not defensible is that `evalf` accepts the same 0 (F5), which is charged there instead. |
| PD000/ED000 | `pi(0)` / `e(0)` | Same as above; listed INCONCLUSIVE in the table rather than as a defect. |
| MFT_1_2 | `evalf(tan(pi(100)*1/2), 40)` | Counted under F11 (the refusal is a defect); it is INCONCLUSIVE only in the automatic numeric comparator, which has no value to compare. |
| -3^2 | `-3^2` -> `-9`, `-3^0` -> `-1`, `-2^3^2` -> `-512` | Operator precedence is not in any contract document. The runner's reading (unary minus binds looser than `^`, `^` is right-associative) is the Python/SymPy reading, so the expectations were corrected to match and the probes are HELD. Flagged only because no document fixes it. |
| F9 evidence | Whether `pi(d)`'s 100-decimal clamp is intentional | No document states a maximum for `pi(digits)` or `e(digits)`. Charged P1 as a silent undocumented clamp; if the maintainer states the cap, this drops to P3. |

Harness corrections made during the audit, recorded so the counts are honest: the first pass generated negative
bases as `-3^2` and expected `(-3)^2`; the runner's `-9` is right and 16 probes were reclassified HELD and
re-tested with explicit parentheses (family F4b, 28/28 HELD). Two more first-pass expectations were simply wrong
(`(1/3+1/6)*6` and `0.1(6)`); both were corrected and are HELD.

## Summary

1323 probe inputs were generated programmatically and evaluated one process each through
`out/aot/Lovelace.Run.exe` at commit `9e761ba`, with the same quantity computed independently by SymPy 1.14.0,
mpmath 1.3.0 and Python `fractions`. **1102 HELD, 215 FINDING, 6 INCONCLUSIVE**; among the findings **145 are P0, 53 P1,
17 P2 and none P3**. The 215 finding probes are 14 distinct defects; all 215 were re-run from clean temp files and
reproduced identically.

The arithmetic that the product is proudest of held up well: 69/78 exact-rational probes, 61/62 decimal and
periodic-literal probes, 50/52 comparisons, 165/165 substitutions, 119/119 integer powers, 28/28 parenthesised
powers, 243/256 trig values at rational multiples of pi, 42/48 generic trig points, 63/69 long-period
denominators and all 20 root probes agreed with ground truth. The failures cluster in three places: (a) the
`exact` flag and the rational payload that `evalf` attaches to symbolic transcendental results - 145 of the 215
finding probes are this one wrong exactness claim, and it also produces genuinely wrong values through
`exp(log(x))`, `exp(1)*exp(-1)`, `sin^2+cos^2` and `sin(k*pi(100))`; (b) the comparison layer, where the exact
rational 1 stored for `0.(9)` is not equal to 1 while its difference from 1 is 0; and (c) the `digits` boundary of
`evalf`, which at 0 raises a NullReferenceException for `sin`/`cos`, returns 0 declared exact for `1/3`, and is
ignored entirely for an already-numeric argument. Every P1 is either a documented bound that the Cycle-5
amendment lists as unclosed and that is still live at this commit (O-B10, O-B14, O-B8) or a new instance of the
same class (`/` versus `*` exactness, the '1/q*q' threshold at q = 1000003, the 100-decimal clamp on
`pi(d)`/`e(d)`).

### P0 titles

- F1 evalf claims exactness and a rational form on truncated transcendentals
- F2 exact identities (exp(log(2)), exp(1)*exp(-1), sin^2+cos^2) fail and are declared exact
- F3 sin/tan at integer multiples of pi(100) return +/-k*10^-30 with the wrong sign, declared exact
- F4 0.(9) is not 1 for ==/< while its payload is 1/1 and 0.(9)-1 == 0
- F5 evalf(f,0) raises InternalInvariantFailure for sin/cos and 0-declared-exact for 1/3
- F6 evalf returns exactly 0 for a nonzero value whose leading zeros exceed the digit budget

### P1 titles

- F7 division discards the exactness the identical multiplication keeps
- F8 (1/q)*q stops being exactly 1 at q >= 1000003 (contradicts O-3a)
- F9 pi(d)/e(d) silently cap at 100 decimals; evalf ignores digits on numeric arguments
- F10 (-4)^(1/2) = 2i succeeds though capabilities() advertises non-integer exponents unsupported
- F11 tan(pi(100)/2) is refused as a division by zero though it is about 2.43e100
